, and is available upon request.

\blacksquare

Lemma C7
.

Under Assumptions
B1
-
B3
, the best
Q_{t}
is given by equation (
10
).

Proof.

The intuition of the construction of
Q_{t}
is motivated by the first-order conditions of the GMM objective function.
From (
1
), we dentoe

\displaystyle A=B_{1}^{-1}(\gamma I_{n}+B_{2})=A_{y}+A_{r}

(C7)

where
A_{y}=S^{-1}(\gamma I_{n}+S_{2})
and
A_{r}=(B_{1}^{-1}-S_{1}^{-1})(\gamma I_{n}+S_{2})=-S_{1}^{-1}R_{1}B_{1}^{-1}(\gamma I_{n}+S_{2})
. It is straightforward to verify that
A_{x}
satisfies
A1
and
A_{r}
satisfies
A1

O_{p}(\ell_{1}^{-\varsigma_{1}})
.
For any
h>0
, we have

\displaystyle Y_{t+h}=A^{h+1}Y_{t-1}+\sum_{j=0}^{h}A^{j}B_{1}^{-1}(X_{t+h-j}\beta+\mathbf{c}_{n}+\alpha_{t+h-j}l_{n})+\sum_{j=0}^{h}A^{j}B_{1}^{-1}B_{3}^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{t+h-j}

(C8)

By denoting

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\chi$}}_{t+h-j}&=\sum_{j=0}^{h}A^{j}B_{1}^{-1}(X_{t+h-j}\beta+\frac{1}{t-1}\sum_{h=1}^{t-1}\mathfrak{C}_{h}+\alpha_{t+h-j}l_{n})\\
E_{t+h-j}&=\sum_{j=0}^{h}A^{j}B_{1}^{-1}B_{3}^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{t+h-j}+\frac{1}{t-1}\sum_{h=0}^{t-1}B_{3}^{-1}\mathcal{E}_{h}\end{split}

where
\mathfrak{C}_{t}=\mathfrak{C}_{y,t}+\mathfrak{C}_{r,t}=B_{1}Y_{t}-(\gamma I_{n}+B_{2})Y_{t-1}-X_{nt}\beta-\alpha_{t}l_{n}
with
\mathfrak{C}_{y,t}=S_{1}Y_{t}-(\gamma I_{n}+S_{2})Y_{t-1}-X_{nt}\beta-\alpha_{t}l_{n}
and
\mathfrak{C}_{r,t}=R_{1}Y_{t}-R_{2}Y_{t-1}
.
We then have

\displaystyle\begin{split}Y_{t-1}^{(*,-1)}&=h_{Tt}(Y_{t-1}-\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{s=t}^{T-1}Y_{s})=h_{Tt}(Y_{t-1}-\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=0}^{T-1-t}Y_{h})\\
&=h_{Tt}\left(I_{n}-\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=0}^{T-1-t}A^{h+1}\right)Y_{t-1}-h_{Tt}\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=0}^{T-1-t}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\chi$}}_{t+h-j}-h_{Tt}\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=0}^{T-1-t}E_{t+h-j}\end{split}

Since we use
A_{y}
to approximate
A
according to (
C7
), we then have

\displaystyle Y_{t-1}^{(*,-1)}=h_{Tt}\left(I_{n}-\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=0}^{T-1-t}A_{y}^{h+1}\right)Y_{t-1}-h_{Tt}\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=0}^{T-1-t}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\chi$}}_{y,t+h-j}-h_{Tt}\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=0}^{T-1-t}\mathscr{Q}_{t+h-j}

(C9)

where

\displaystyle\mathscr{Q}_{t+h-j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{r}^{h+1}Y_{t-1}+{\scalebox{1.2}{$\chi$}}_{r,t+h-j}+E_{t+h-j}

\displaystyle{\scalebox{1.2}{$\chi$}}_{y,t+h-j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{h}A_{y}^{j}S_{1}^{-1}(X_{t+h-j}\beta+\frac{1}{t-1}\sum_{h=1}^{t-1}\mathfrak{C}_{y,h}+\alpha_{t+h-j}l_{n})

\displaystyle{\scalebox{1.2}{$\chi$}}_{r,t+h-j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{h}A_{r}^{j}B_{1}^{-1}(X_{t+h-j}\beta+\frac{1}{t-1}\sum_{h=1}^{t-1}\mathfrak{C}_{h}+\alpha_{t+h-j}l_{n})+\sum_{j=0}^{h}A_{y}^{j}R_{1}^{-1}(X_{t+h-j}\beta+\frac{1}{t-1}\sum_{h=1}^{t-1}\mathfrak{C}_{h}+\alpha_{t+h-j}l_{n})

(C10)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{h}A_{y}^{j}S_{1}^{-1}\frac{1}{t-1}\sum_{h=1}^{t-1}\mathfrak{C}_{r,h}

Denote
\mathcal{I}_{t-1}
be the sigma-algebra spanned
by
(Y_{0},...,Y_{t-1})
conditional on
(X_{1},...X_{T},\mathbf{c}_{0},\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{T0})
and
\tilde{\mathscr{Q}}_{tT}=\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=0}^{T-1-t}\mathscr{Q}_{t+h-j}
, from (
C9
), we have

\displaystyle Y_{t-1}^{(*,-1)}=\mathbb{Y}_{t}-h_{Tt}\tilde{\mathscr{Q}}_{tT}

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{Y}_{t}\equiv\operatorname{E}[Y_{t-1}^{(*,-1)}|\mathcal{I}_{t-1}]=h_{Tt}\left(I_{n}-\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=0}^{T-1-t}A_{y}^{h+1}\right)Y_{t-1}-h_{Tt}\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=0}^{T-1-t}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\chi$}}_{y,t+h-j}.

(C11)

We can show that
\|\tilde{\mathscr{Q}}_{tT}\|=O_{p}(\sqrt{n}\ell_{n}^{-\underline{\varsigma}})
, which has the same asymptotic behavior as
\|r_{t}\|
, and thus is asymptotically negligible with respect to the estimators.
This is because
\|A_{r}^{h+1}Y_{t-1}\|\leq\|A_{r}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}^{h+1}\|Y_{t-1}\|=O_{p}(\ell_{n}^{-(h+1)\underline{\varsigma}})O_{p}(\sqrt{n})=o_{p}(\sqrt{n}\ell_{n}^{-\underline{\varsigma}})
,
\|\sum_{j=0}^{h}A_{r}^{j}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O_{p}(\ell_{n}^{-\underline{\varsigma}})
,
\|\mathfrak{C}_{r,t}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O_{p}(\ell_{n}^{-\underline{\varsigma}})
and
\|X_{t}\beta+\mathbf{c}_{n}+\alpha_{t}l_{n}\|=O_{p}(\sqrt{n})
by Assumptions
B2
and
B3
.

Similarly, for some time-invariant
n\times n
matrix
\mathcal{B}_{k}
satisfying
A1
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{k}\mathbb{Y}_{t}\equiv\operatorname{E}[\mathcal{B}_{k}Y_{t-1}^{(*,-1)}|\mathcal{I}_{t-1}]=h_{Tt}\mathcal{B}_{k}\bigl(I_{n}-\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=0}^{T-1-t}A_{y}^{h+1}\bigr)Y_{t-1}-h_{Tt}\mathcal{B}_{k}\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=0}^{T-1-t}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\chi$}}_{y,t+h-j}

(C12)

Therefore, according to equation (
C13
),
Q_{t}
now is in (
10
).

\blacksquare

C.2  Proof of Theorems

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.
We establish the consistency and asymptotic normality of the OGMME in this section. The corresponding results for the BGMME follow analogously, by replacing
\mathbf{J}_{N}
with
\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})
.

(i) From Lemma
S11
in the supplement, the first-order condition at
\theta_{0}
times
n(T-1)
is approximated by
A7
A7

A7

Note that
r_{t}^{*}
also contains
\lambda
, however, it is easy to verify that the order of
\|\frac{\partial r_{t}^{*}}{\partial\lambda_{j}^{\prime}}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O_{p}(\ell_{n}^{-\underline{\varsigma}})
by Assumption
B2
, which is negligible. Also, we need to divide
n(T-1)
to investigate the consistency and divide
\sqrt{n(T-1)}
to investigate the asymptotical normality. As shown in Lemma
S11
, the approximation terms will be negligible.

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\partial m_{N}(\theta_{0})}{\partial\lambda_{1}}&\approx-\begin{pmatrix}\left(\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{1N}}{\partial\lambda_{1}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{*}\right)^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{N1}^{s}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\\
\vdots\\
\left(\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{1N}}{\partial\lambda_{1}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{*}\right)^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{N\ell_{p}}^{s}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{1N}}{\partial\lambda_{1}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{*}\end{pmatrix},\frac{\partial m_{N}(\theta_{0})}{\partial\lambda_{3}}\approx\begin{pmatrix}\left(\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{3N}}{\partial\lambda_{3}}\mathbf{S}_{3N}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\right)^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{N1}^{s}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\\
\vdots\\
\left(\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{3N}}{\partial\lambda_{3}}\mathbf{S}_{3N}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\right)^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{N\ell_{p}}^{s}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{3N}}{\partial\lambda_{3}}\mathbf{S}_{3N}^{-1}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\end{pmatrix},\\
\frac{\partial m_{N}(\theta_{0})}{\partial\lambda_{2}}&\approx-\begin{pmatrix}\left(\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{2N}}{\partial\lambda_{2}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{(*,-1)}\right)^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{N1}^{s}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\\
\vdots\\
\left(\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{2N}}{\partial\lambda_{2}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{(*,-1)}\right)^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{N\ell_{p}}^{s}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\left(\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{2N}}{\lambda_{2\ell_{2}}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{(*,-1)}\right)\end{pmatrix},\frac{\partial m_{N}(\theta_{0})}{\partial\pi^{\prime}}\approx-\begin{pmatrix}(\mathbf{S}_{3N}\mathbf{Z}_{N}^{*})^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{N1}^{s}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\\
\vdots\\
(\mathbf{S}_{3N}\mathbf{Z}_{N}^{*})^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{N\ell_{p}}^{s}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{S}_{3N}\mathbf{Z}_{N}^{*}\end{pmatrix}\end{split}

(C13)

where
\mathbf{Z}_{N}^{*}=[\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{(*,-1)},\mathbf{X}_{N}^{*}]
.

From the Taylor expansion, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{gmm}-\theta_{0}={}

\displaystyle-\Big[\frac{\partial m_{N}^{\prime}(\hat{\theta}_{gmm})}{\partial\theta}\Omega_{N}^{-1}\frac{\partial m_{N}(\bar{\theta}^{\prime})}{\partial\theta}\Big]^{-1}\frac{\partial m_{N}^{\prime}(\hat{\theta}_{gmm})}{\partial\theta}\Omega_{N}^{-1}\frac{1}{n(T-1)}m_{N}(\theta_{0})

(C14)

and by Lemma
S8
in the supplement, we have

\frac{\partial m_{N}^{\prime}(\hat{\theta}_{gmm})}{\partial\theta}=D_{N}+O(\ell_{n}^{-\underline{\varsigma}})+o_{p}(1)
, where

\displaystyle D_{N}=-\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\operatorname{E}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{\ell_{p}\times\ell_{\pi}}&\mathbf{C}_{\ell_{p}\times\ell_{1}}&\mathbf{0}_{\ell_{p}\times\ell_{2}}&\mathbf{C}_{\ell_{p}\times\ell_{3}}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{S}_{3N}\mathbf{Z}_{N}^{*}&\mathbf{Q}_{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{1N}}{\lambda_{1\ell_{1}}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{*}&\mathbf{Q}_{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{2N}}{\lambda_{2\ell_{2}}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{(*,-1)}&\mathbf{0}_{\ell_{q}\times\ell_{3}}\end{pmatrix},

(C15)

and we denote

\hskip-28.45274pt\begin{split}D_{\pi}=-\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\operatorname{E}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{\ell_{p}\times\ell_{\pi}}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{Z}_{N}^{*}\end{pmatrix}\ \text{and}\ D_{\lambda}=-\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\operatorname{E}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{C}_{\ell_{p}\times\ell_{1}}&\mathbf{0}_{\ell_{p}\times\ell_{2}}&\mathbf{C}_{\ell_{p}\times\ell_{3}}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{1N}}{\lambda_{1\ell_{1}}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{*}&\mathbf{Q}_{N}^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{2N}}{\lambda_{2\ell_{2}}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{(*,-1)}&\mathbf{0}_{\ell_{q}\times\ell_{3}}\end{pmatrix}.\end{split}

Furthermore,

[\mathbf{C}_{\ell_{p}\times\ell_{1}}]_{jk}=\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{Nj}^{s}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\mathbf{S}_{1N}}{\partial\lambda_{1k}}\mathbf{S}_{1N}^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{3N}^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\bigr)

and

[\mathbf{C}_{\ell_{p}\times\ell_{3}}]_{jk}=\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{Nj}^{s}\mathbf{J}_{N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{3N}}{\partial\lambda_{3k}}\mathbf{S}_{3N}^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\bigr)
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial m_{N}^{\prime}(\hat{\theta}_{gmm})}{\partial\theta}\Omega_{N}^{-1}\frac{1}{n(T-1)}m_{N}(\theta_{0})=\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\mathbf{C}_{\ell_{p}\times\ell_{1}}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Omega}_{1N}^{-1}\mathcal{J}_{1}+\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\mathbf{C}_{\ell_{p}\times\ell_{3}}^{\prime}\mathbf{\Omega}_{1N}^{-1}\mathcal{J}_{1}+\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\mathcal{J}_{2}+o_{p}(1)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{1}=-\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{N1}^{s}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\\
\vdots\\
\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{N\ell_{p}}^{s}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\\
\mathbf{0}_{\ell_{q}\times 1}\end{pmatrix},\ \text{and}\ \mathcal{J}_{2}=-\mathbf{L}_{N}^{*\prime}\mathbf{M}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*},

(C16)

with

\mathbf{L}_{N}^{*}=\big[\mathbf{S}_{3N}\mathbf{Z}_{N}^{*},\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{1N}}{\partial\lambda_{1}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{*},\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{2N}}{\partial\lambda_{2}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{(*,-1)},\mathbf{0}_{N\times\ell_{3}}\big]
as defined in Notation
A1
(v),

\mathbf{M}_{N}=\mathtt{Diag}_{t=1}^{T-1}(M_{t})
and
M_{t}=J_{n}Q_{t}(Q_{t}^{\prime}J_{n}\Sigma_{t}J_{n}Q_{t})^{-1}Q_{t}^{\prime}J_{n}
.

Next, due to
\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{(*,-1)^{\prime}}\mathbf{M}_{N}\mathbf{E}_{N}^{*}=\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{T-t}{T-t+1}(Y_{t}-\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=t}^{T-1}Y_{h})^{\prime}M_{t}(E_{t}-\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=t+1}^{T-1}E_{t})
,
and we can deduce that the non-zero expectation part of is
\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{1}{T-t+1}(\kappa_{1\gamma}+\kappa_{2\gamma})
, where

\kappa_{1\gamma,t}=-E_{t}^{\prime}M_{t}B_{3}(\sum_{h=1}^{T-t}A^{h-1})B_{1}^{-1}B_{3}^{-1}E_{t}\ \text{and}\ \kappa_{2\gamma,t}=\frac{1}{(T-t)}\big[\sum_{h=t+1}^{T-1}E_{h}^{\prime}M_{t}(\sum_{s=1}^{T-h}A^{s-1})B_{1}^{-1}B_{3}^{-1}E_{t}\big].

By the triangle inequality, we have

\big\|\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{(*,-1)^{\prime}}\mathbf{M}_{N}\mathbf{E}_{N}^{*}\big\|\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{1}{T-t+1}\big(\|\kappa_{1\gamma}\|+\|\kappa_{2\gamma}\|\big).

Note that

\operatorname{E}\|\kappa_{1\gamma,t}\|=O\left(\ell_{n}\right)\ \text{and}\ \operatorname{E}\|\kappa_{2\gamma,t}\|=O\left(\ell_{n}\right)

since
\|M_{t}(\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}A^{h})B_{1}^{-1}B_{3}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{rc}}=O_{p}(\ell_{n})
from Lemma
S7
in the supplement.
By Markov’s inequality and the fact that the magnitude order of
\sum_{h=1}^{T-1}\frac{T-t}{T-t+1}
is
O(T-1)
, we have

\Big\|\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{(*,-1)^{\prime}}\mathbf{M}_{N}\mathbf{E}_{N}^{*}\Big\|=O_{p}\left(\ell_{n}\cdot\frac{\ln{T}}{n(T-1)}\right).

Hence, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{(*,-1)\prime}\mathbf{M}_{N}\mathbf{r}_{N}^{*}\Big\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{n(T-1)\ell_{n}^{-\underline{\varsigma}}}{n(T-1)}\cdot\ell_{n}\right)=O_{p}\left(\ell_{n}^{1-\underline{\varsigma}}\right)

(C17)

since the relationship between
\mathbf{r}_{N}
and
\mathbf{E}_{N}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\left(\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{2N}}{\partial\lambda_{2}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{(*,-1)}\right)^{\prime}\mathbf{M}_{N}\mathbf{E}_{N}^{*}\Big\|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\ell_{n}}\cdot\frac{\ell_{n}\ln{T}}{n(T-1)}\right)=O_{p}\left(\frac{\ell_{n}^{3/2}\ln{T}}{n(T-1)}\right)\ \text{and}\ \\
&\Big\|\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\left(\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{2N}}{\partial\lambda_{2}}\mathbf{Y}_{N}^{(*,-1)}\right)^{\prime}\mathbf{M}_{N}\mathbf{r}_{N}^{*}\Big\|=O_{p}(\ell_{n})\cdot O_{p}(\sqrt{\ell_{n}})\cdot O_{p}(\ell_{n}^{-\underline{\varsigma}})=O_{p}\left(\ell_{n}^{3/2-\underline{\varsigma}}\right)\end{split}

since
\|\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{2N}}{\partial\lambda_{2}}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(\sqrt{\ell_{n}})
.
For the last two terms in linear moments, we have
\Big\|\frac{1}{n(T-1)}(\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{1N}}{\partial\lambda_{1}}\mathbf{E}_{N}^{*})^{\prime}\mathbf{M}_{N}\mathbf{E}_{N}^{*}\Big\|=O_{p}(\frac{\ell_{n}^{3/2}}{n})
, and

\Big\|\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\mathbf{X}_{N}^{*\prime}\mathbf{M}_{N}\mathbf{E}_{N}^{*}\Big\|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\ell_{n}}{n(T-1)}}\right)
.
Finally, from Lemma
S8
in the supplement, for
j=1,...,\ell_{p}
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\mathbf{r}_{N}^{*\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{\!Nj}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{r}_{N}^{*}\Big\|=O_{p}(\ell_{n}^{-2\underline{\varsigma}}),\ \text{and}\ \Big\|\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\mathbf{r}_{N}^{*\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{\!Nj}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{E}_{N}^{*}\Big\|=O_{p}(\ell_{n}^{-\underline{\varsigma}})=o_{p}(\ell_{n}^{1-\underline{\varsigma}}).

(C18)

Thus, the bias of the quadratic moments is

O_{p}(\ell_{n}^{-2\underline{\varsigma}}\cdot\ell_{n})=O_{p}(\ell_{n}^{1-2\underline{\varsigma}})=o_{p}(\ell_{n}^{3/2-\underline{\varsigma}}).

Consistency of
\hat{\pi}_{gmm}

We use the
C(\alpha)
approach (see e.g.
Jin and Lee 2020
;
Chen et al. 2025
) to derive the expansion of
\hat{\pi}_{gmm}
and
\hat{\lambda}_{gmm}

from (
C14
).
Define the projection matrix
M_{\lambda}=I_{\ell_{m}}-D_{\lambda}(D_{\lambda}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}D_{\lambda})^{-1}D_{\lambda}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}
and
M_{\pi}=I_{\ell_{m}}-D_{\pi}(D_{\pi}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}D_{\pi})^{-1}D_{\pi}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}
where
\ell_{m}=\ell_{p}+\ell_{q}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\hat{\pi}_{gmm}-\pi_{0}=-[D_{\pi}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}M_{\lambda}D_{\pi}]^{-1}D_{\pi}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}M_{\lambda}\frac{1}{n(T-1)}m_{N}(\theta_{0})+o_{p}(1)

(C19)

\displaystyle\hat{\lambda}_{gmm}-\lambda_{0}=-[D_{\lambda}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}M_{\pi}D_{\lambda}]^{-1}D_{\lambda}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}M_{\pi}\frac{1}{n(T-1)}m_{N}(\theta_{0})+o_{p}(1)

(C20)

Let
\tilde{D}_{\lambda}=\Omega_{N}^{-\frac{1}{2}}D_{\lambda}

and

\tilde{D}_{\pi}=\Omega_{N}^{-\frac{1}{2}}D_{\pi}
,
and define

\tilde{M}_{\lambda}=\Omega_{N}^{-\frac{1}{2}}M_{\lambda}\Omega_{N}^{-\frac{1}{2}}=I_{\ell_{m}}-\tilde{D}_{\lambda}(\tilde{D}_{\lambda}^{\prime}\tilde{D}_{\lambda})^{-1}\tilde{D}_{\lambda}^{\prime}
and

\tilde{M}_{\pi}=\Omega_{N}^{-\frac{1}{2}}M_{\pi}\Omega_{N}^{-\frac{1}{2}}=I_{\ell_{m}}-\tilde{D}_{\pi}(\tilde{D}_{\pi}^{\prime}\tilde{D}_{\pi})^{-1}\tilde{D}_{\pi}^{\prime}
. Each
\tilde{M}_{\bullet}
is the orthogonal projector and thus
\|\tilde{M}_{\bullet}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=1
where the subscript
\bullet
denotes
\pi
or
\lambda
. Then, we have
M_{\bullet}\leq\|\Omega_{N}^{1/2}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|\tilde{M}_{\bullet}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|\Omega_{N}^{1/2}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(1)
. Define

\displaystyle K_{\pi}=-[D_{\pi}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}M_{\lambda}D_{\pi}]^{-1}D_{\pi}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}M_{\lambda}\ \text{and}\ K_{\lambda}=-[D_{\lambda}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}M_{\pi}D_{\lambda}]^{-1}D_{\lambda}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}M_{\pi},

(C21)

We have
\|K_{\pi}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\Big\|[D_{\lambda}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}M_{\pi}D_{\lambda}]^{-1}\Big\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\Big\|D_{\lambda}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}M_{\pi}\Big\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(1)
by Assumption
B5
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hat{\pi}_{gmm}-\pi_{0}=K_{\pi}\frac{1}{n(T-1)}m_{N}(\theta_{0})=K_{\pi}\frac{1}{n(T-1)}m_{\Delta}+O_{p}(\|\mathcal{J}_{1}\|)+O_{p}(\|\mathcal{J}_{2}\|)\end{split}

(C22)

where

\mathcal{J}_{i}
’s are in (
C16
) for
i=1,2
, and
m_{\Delta}
is in (
C2
).
Therefore, combining (
C17
)-(
C18
), we have

\displaystyle\hat{\pi}_{gmm}-\pi_{0}=O_{p}\Bigl(\ell_{n}^{3/2-\underline{\varsigma}}+\frac{\ell_{n}^{3/2}}{n}+\frac{\ell_{n}^{3/2}\ln{T}}{n(T-1)}+\sqrt{\frac{\ell_{n}}{n(T-1)}}\Bigr)=O_{p}\Bigl(\ell_{n}^{3/2-\underline{\varsigma}}+\frac{\ell_{n}^{3/2}}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\ell_{n}}{n(T-1)}}\Bigr).

(C23)

(ii)
For the limiting distribution, define

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\pi_{0},gmm}=\lim_{n,T\to\infty}K_{\pi}\Omega_{N}K_{\pi}^{\prime},

(C24)

and then
\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\pi_{0},gmm}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\|K_{\pi}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}^{2}\|\Omega_{N}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(1)
.
By (
C22
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sqrt{n(T-1)}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\pi_{0},gmm}^{-1/2}(\hat{\pi}_{gmm}-\pi_{0})&=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\pi_{0},gmm}^{-1/2}K_{\pi}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n(T-1)}}m_{\Delta}+O_{p}\left(\sqrt{n(T-1)}\Bigl(\ell_{n}^{3/2-\underline{\varsigma}}+\frac{\ell_{n}^{3/2}}{n}\Bigr)\right).\end{split}

(C25)

It remain to show that
\sqrt{n(T-1)}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\pi_{0},gmm}^{-1/2}(\hat{\pi}_{gmm}-\pi_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N(0,I_{\ell_{\pi}})
.
The results are directly obtained from Lemma
C4
and the Cramér–Wold device.

\blacksquare

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.
For (i),
define
\Sigma_{\pi,N}=K_{\pi}\Omega_{N}K_{\pi}^{\prime}
, then

\bm{\Sigma}_{\pi_{0},gmm}=\lim_{n,T\to\infty}\Sigma_{\pi,N}
.
It suffices to show (a)
\|\hat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\pi,gmm}-\Sigma_{\pi,N}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=o_{p}(1)
and
(b)
\|\Sigma_{\pi,N}-\bm{\Sigma}_{\pi_{0},gmm}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=o_{p}(1)
.

For (a), note that

\hat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\pi,gmm}-\Sigma_{\pi,N}=(\hat{H}_{\pi}-K_{\pi})\hat{\Omega}_{N}\hat{H}_{\pi}^{\prime}+K_{\pi}(\hat{\Omega}_{N}-\Omega_{N})\hat{H}_{\pi}^{\prime}+K_{\pi}\Omega_{N}(\hat{H}_{\pi}^{\prime}-K_{\pi}^{\prime}).

Taking spectral norms and using submultiplicativity yields

\|\hat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\pi,gmm}-\Sigma_{\pi,N}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\|\hat{H}_{\pi}-K_{\pi}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|\hat{\Omega}_{N}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|\hat{H}_{\pi}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}+\|K_{\pi}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|\hat{\Omega}_{N}-\Omega_{N}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|\hat{H}_{\pi}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}+\|K_{\pi}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|\Omega_{N}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|\hat{H}_{\pi}-K_{\pi}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}.

Under Assumption
B5
,
K_{\pi}
and
\Omega_{N}
are
O(1)
.
By Lemma
C3
,\|\hat{D}_{\pi}-D_{\pi}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=o_{p}(1)
and
\|\hat{D}_{\lambda}-D_{\lambda}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=o_{p}(1)
.
Therefore,
\|\hat{H}_{\pi}-K_{\pi}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=o_{p}(1)
since
\|\hat{\Omega}_{N}-\Omega_{N}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=o_{p}(1)
by Lemma
C6
,
which implies
\|\hat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\pi,gmm}-\Sigma_{\pi,N}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=o_{p}(1)
.
For (b), it holds by the definition
\bm{\Sigma}_{\pi_{0},gmm}=\lim_{n,T\to\infty}K_{\pi}\Omega_{N}K_{\pi}^{\prime}
.
Combining (a) and (b) completes the proof.

For (ii),
denote
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{b_{\pi}}=\min\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\pi_{0},gmm}=\min\{\lim_{n,T\to\infty}K_{\pi}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}K_{\pi}\}
.
The equation (
C24
) can be equivalently written as
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\pi_{0},gmm}=\mathtt{e}_{\pi}(\lim_{n,T\to\infty}D_{N}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}D_{N})^{-1}\mathtt{e}_{\pi}^{\prime}
, where
\mathtt{e}_{\pi}
is the
\ell_{\pi}\times\ell_{\theta}
selection matrix that extracts the first
\ell_{\pi}
components, i.e.,
\mathtt{e}_{\pi}\theta=\pi
.
In the case of BGMME,
\mathbf{J}_{N}
is replaced by
\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})
, and accordingly,
\mathbf{M}_{N}=\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})\mathbf{Q}_{N}(\mathbf{Q}_{N}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})\mathbf{Q}_{N})^{-1}\mathbf{Q}_{N}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})
. For the quadratic moments, we have
\ell_{p}=\ell_{*}=\ell_{1}+\ell_{3}
, and the components in
\mathbf{C}_{\ell_{*}\times\ell_{1}}
and
\mathbf{C}_{\ell_{*}\times\ell_{3}}
, are

[\mathbf{C}_{\ell_{*}\times\ell_{1}}]_{jk}=\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})\mathbf{P}_{Nj}^{s}\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\mathbf{S}_{1N}}{\partial\lambda_{1k}}\mathbf{S}_{1N}^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{3N}^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\bigr)

and

[\mathbf{C}_{\ell_{*}\times\ell_{3}}]_{jk}=\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})\mathbf{P}_{Nj}^{s}\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})\tfrac{\partial\mathbf{S}_{3N}}{\partial\lambda_{3k}}\mathbf{S}_{3N}^{-1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\bigr)
, respectively.
Note that

\begin{split}[\mathbf{C}_{\ell_{*}\times\ell_{1}}]_{jk}=&\operatorname{tr}\bigl((\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\mathbf{S}_{1N}}{\partial\lambda_{1k}}\mathbf{S}_{1N}^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{3N}^{-1})^{\prime}\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})\mathbf{P}_{Nj}^{s}\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})\bigr)\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\bigl([\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\mathbf{S}_{1N}}{\partial\lambda_{1k}}\mathbf{S}_{1N}^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{3N}^{-1})^{s}[\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{\frac{1}{2}}[\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{P}_{Nj}^{s}[\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigr)\\
=&\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathtt{vec}^{\prime}\Bigl([\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\mathbf{S}_{3N}\tfrac{\mathbf{S}_{1N}}{\partial\lambda_{1k}}\mathbf{S}_{1N}^{-1}\mathbf{S}_{3N}^{-1})^{s}[\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr)\right\}\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathtt{vec}\Bigl([\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{P}_{Nj}^{s}[\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigr)\right\}\\
=&a_{1j}^{\prime}a_{2k}\end{split}

by using the symmetry of
\mathbf{P}_{Nj}^{s}
and
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}
, together with the identity
\operatorname{tr}(A)=1/2\operatorname{tr}(A^{s})
, and
\operatorname{tr}(AB)=\mathtt{vec}^{\prime}(A)\mathtt{vec}(B)
for any conformable matrices
A
and
B
, where
\mathtt{vec}(\cdot)
stacks all entries of a matrix into a column vector.
Similarly,

[\mathbf{C}_{\ell_{*}\times\ell_{3}}]_{jk}=a_{3j}^{\prime}a_{2k}
,
where
a_{3j}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathtt{vec}\bigl([\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{N}\tfrac{\mathbf{S}_{3N}}{\partial\lambda_{3k}}\mathbf{S}_{3N}^{-1})^{s}[\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigr)

and the components in
\mathbf{\Omega}_{N1}
is

[\mathbf{\Omega}_{N1}]_{ij}=\operatorname{tr}(\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})\mathbf{P}_{Ni}\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})\mathbf{P}_{Nj}^{s}\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}}))=1/2\operatorname{tr}([\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{P}_{Nj}^{s}[\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{\frac{1}{2}}[\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{P}_{Nj}^{s}[\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{\frac{1}{2}})=a_{2j}^{\prime}a_{2j}.

Hence,
D_{N}^{\prime}\Omega_{N}^{-1}D_{N}=(\operatorname{E}\Sigma_{\texttt{line}}+\operatorname{E}\Sigma_{\texttt{quad}})
, where

\Sigma_{\texttt{line}}=\lim_{n,T\to\infty}\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\mathbf{L}_{N}^{*^{\prime}}\mathbf{M}_{N}\mathbf{L}_{N}^{*}=\lim_{n,T\to\infty}\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\mathbf{L}_{N}^{*^{\prime}}[\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{1/2}[\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{1/2}\mathbf{Q}_{N}(\mathbf{Q}_{N}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})\mathbf{Q}_{N})^{-1}\mathbf{Q}_{N}^{\prime}[\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{1/2}[\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})]^{1/2}\mathbf{L}_{N}^{*}\leq\lim_{n,T\to\infty}\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\mathbf{L}_{N}^{*^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})\mathbf{L}_{N}^{*}

and

\Sigma_{\texttt{quad}}=\lim_{n,T\to\infty}\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\kappa_{\lambda}^{\prime}\kappa_{\delta}(\kappa_{\delta}^{\prime}\kappa_{\delta})^{-1}\kappa_{\delta}^{\prime}\kappa_{\lambda}\leq\lim_{n,T\to\infty}\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\kappa_{\lambda}^{\prime}\kappa_{\lambda}

by the Generalized Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, where
\kappa_{\lambda}=(a_{11},...,a_{1\ell_{1}},a_{31},...,a_{3\ell_{3}})^{\prime}
and

\kappa_{\delta}=(a_{21},...,a_{2\ell_{*}})^{\prime}
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\pi_{0},bgmm}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{b_{\pi}}=\mathtt{e}_{\pi}\left(\lim_{n,T\to\infty}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\theta}\right)^{-1}\mathtt{e}_{\pi}^{\prime}

(C26)

where

\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\theta}=\operatorname{E}\Bigl(\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\mathbf{L}_{N}^{*^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{J}({\bm{\Sigma}_{N}})\mathbf{L}_{N}^{*}+\mathtt{Diag}(\kappa_{\theta}^{\prime}\kappa_{\theta})\Bigr)
with
\kappa_{\theta}=(\mathbf{0}_{\ell_{\pi}\times 1},a_{11},\dots,a_{1\ell_{1}},\mathbf{0}_{\ell_{2}\times 1},a_{31},\dots,a_{3\ell_{3}})^{\prime}
.
Then, we can evaluate
\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{b_{\pi}}
by
\pi_{bgmm}
. Its consistency follows by similar arguments to those presented in (i).

\blacksquare

Proof of Theorem
4.3
.
Because

|\hat{g}_{k,gmm}(d)-g_{k0}(d)|\leq|\hat{g}_{k,gmm}(d)-\xi_{k0}(d)|+|g_{k0}(d)-\xi_{k0}(d)|
.
Consequently, taking the supremum over
d\in D
yields

\displaystyle\sup_{d\in D}|\hat{g}_{k,gmm}(d)-g_{k0}(d)|\leq\sup_{d\in D}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{k}(d)\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|\hat{\lambda}_{k,gmm}-\lambda_{k_{0}}\|+O_{p}(\ell_{n}^{-\underline{\varsigma}}).

According to (
C20
) and (
C21
), we know that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hat{\lambda}_{k,gmm}-\lambda_{k_{0}}=K_{\lambda}\frac{1}{n(T-1)}(\mathcal{J}_{1}+\mathcal{J}_{2}),\end{split}

(C27)

combining Assumption
2
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{d\in D}|\hat{g}_{k,gmm}(d)-g_{k0}(d)|&=O_{p}\Bigl(\ell_{n}^{1/2}\Bigl(\ell_{n}^{3/2-\underline{\varsigma}}+\frac{\ell_{n}^{3/2}}{n}+\sqrt{\frac{\ell_{n}}{n(T-1)}}\Bigr)+\ell_{n}^{-\underline{\varsigma}}\Bigr)\\
&=O_{p}\Bigl(\ell_{n}^{2-\underline{\varsigma}}+\frac{\ell_{n}^{2}}{n}+\frac{\ell_{n}}{\sqrt{n(T-1)}}\Bigr).\end{split}

(C28)

Next, let

\displaystyle\Sigma_{g_{k0},gmm}=\lim_{n,T\to\infty}\frac{1}{\ell_{n}}\phi_{k}^{\prime}(d)\Sigma_{\pi_{0},gmm}\phi_{k}(d).

(C29)

Using Assumption
2
(ii), we bound the spectral norm as:

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{g_{k0},gmm}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\dfrac{1}{\ell_{n}}\|\boldsymbol{\phi}_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}^{2}\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\pi_{0},gmm}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=\ell_{n}^{-1}\cdot O(\ell_{n})=O(1).

Combing equations (
C27
) and (
C28
), the sufficient condition for the CLT is

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{(T-1)\ell_{n}^{3}}{n}}+\sqrt{n(T-1)}\ell_{n}^{3/2-\underline{\varsigma}}\to 0

as
(n,T)\to\infty
.

\blacksquare

The sufficient rate conditions differ across estimands.
Theorem
4.1
(i) establishes consistency of the finite-dimensional parameter
\hat{\pi}_{gmm}
using the expansion in (
C19
) evaluated at
\theta_{0}
, and thus only requires

\ell_{n}^{3/2-\underline{\varsigma}}+\ell_{n}^{3/2}/n\to 0
, together with
\sqrt{\tfrac{\ell_{n}}{n(T-1)}}\to 0
as it appears in Assumption
2
(i).
In contrast, Lemma
C3
(ii) imposes the stronger condition

\ell_{n}^{2-\underline{\varsigma}}+\tfrac{\ell_{n}^{2}}{n}+\tfrac{\ell_{n}}{\sqrt{n(T-1)}}\to 0

to guarantee uniform convergence of
\Theta_{N}=\Gamma\times\Lambda_{N}
, which is needed for joint consistency of
\hat{\theta}=(\hat{\pi}^{\prime},\hat{\lambda}^{\prime})^{\prime}
.
Theorem
4.3
(i) requires the same stronger condition to obtain uniform consistency of
\hat{g}_{k,gmm}(d)
over
d\in D
.
The gap reflects the additional difficulty of controlling stochastic fluctuations uniformly over the growing-dimensional sieve space.

Supplementart material

A Supplement to

Semi-nonparametric estimation of spatial dynamic panel data models with nonparametric spatial weights

Appendix S1
Additional lemmas

Lemma S1
.

Let
A
and
B
be
n\times n
real matrices, then
\|AB\|\leq\|A\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|B\|
or
\|AB\|\leq\|B\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|A\|
.

Proof.

This is a standard matrix norm inequality.

\blacksquare

Lemma S2
.

(i) For any time-varying square matrix
\mathcal{B}_{t}
, if

\sup\limits_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{1}=\sup\limits_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\infty}=O(1)
, then
\sup\limits_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(1)
.

(ii) For any time-varying square random matrix
\mathcal{B}_{t}
, depending on an index
n
. If

\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{1}=O_{p}(1)

and

\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}1)
,
then
\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O_{p}(1)
.

Proof.

(i)
We use the standard matrix norm inequality
\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\sqrt{\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{1}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\infty}}
. Taking the supremum over
t
yields:

\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\sup_{t}\sqrt{\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{1}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\infty}}\leq\sqrt{\left(\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{1}\right)\left(\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\infty}\right)}=O(1).

(ii)
According to the proof of (i),
let
X=\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{1}
and
Y=\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\infty}
. Then,
Z=XY=O_{p}(1)
. Since norms are non-negative,
Z\geq 0
. Therefore,
\sqrt{Z}=\sqrt{XY}=O_{p}(1)
. Hence,
0\leq\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\sqrt{XY}=O_{p}(1)
.

\blacksquare

Lemma S3
.

Let the
n\times n
matrix
A
with its typical element
a_{ij}
satisfy

\sup_{i,j}|a_{ij}|=O_{p}(d^{-\nu})
and

\sup_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|a_{ij}|=O_{p}(d^{-\nu})
for some
\nu>0
. Suppose that
n^{1/2}d^{-\nu}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, then:

\|e^{A}-I\|=O_{p}(\|A\|)=O_{p}(n^{1/2}d^{-\nu}).

Proof.
First, it is easy to conclude that
\|A\|=O_{p}(n^{1/2}d^{-\nu})
since
\sum_{j=1}^{n}|a_{ij}|^{2}\leq\left(\sup_{k}|a_{ik}|\right)\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}|a_{ij}|\right)\leq O_{p}(d^{-2\nu})
. Therefore,
\|A\|^{2}=O_{p}(nd^{-2\nu})
.
Second,
Using the triangle inequality for the Frobenius norm and the submultiplicative property, we get:

\|e^{A}-I\|\leq\|A\|+\frac{\|A^{2}\|}{2!}+\frac{\|A^{3}\|}{3!}+\cdots
.
Since
\|A^{k}\|\leq\|A\|^{k}
, this becomes:

\|e^{A}-I\|\leq\|A\|+\frac{\|A\|^{2}}{2!}+\frac{\|A\|^{3}}{3!}+\cdots=e^{\|A\|}-1
.
Since
\|A^{k}\|\leq\|A\|^{k}
, this becomes:

\|e^{A}-I\|\leq\|A\|+\frac{\|A\|^{2}}{2!}+\frac{\|A\|^{3}}{3!}+\cdots=e^{\|A\|}-1
.
Let
X_{p}=\|A\|
,
we have
e^{X_{p}}-1=X_{p}+o_{p}(1)
.
Combining above all, we conclude that
\|e^{A}-I\|=O_{p}(\|A\|)
.

\blacksquare

Lemma
S3
shows that the MESS-type has the same asymptotic behavior as that of the SAR-type.

Lemma S4
.

When
T\to\infty
,

\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}h_{Tt}^{2}=O(T)
and
\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}(1-h_{Tt}^{2})=O(\ln{T})
, where
h_{Tt}=\sqrt{\frac{T-t}{T-t+1}}
.

Proof.

Note that
h_{Tt}^{2}=1-\frac{1}{T-t+1}
, and we have
\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}h_{Tt}^{2}=O(T)-O(\ln{T})=O(T)
since
\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{1}{T-t+1}=O(\ln{T})
.

\blacksquare

Lemma S5
.

Under Assumption
B2
and
\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}}+n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
:

(i)
e^{\Delta_{k}}-I_{n}
satisfies
A1

O_{p}(\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}})
, where
\Delta_{k}
is defined in Notation
A1
(ii). Also,
R_{k}
and
S_{k}R_{k}
satisfy
A1

O_{p}(\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}})
, and
B_{k}
and
S_{k}
satisfy
A1
;

(ii)

H_{k}
satisfies
A1

O_{p}(\ell_{3}^{-\varsigma_{3}})
, where
H_{k}=R_{k}B_{k}^{-1}
;

(iii)

\operatorname{E}\|r_{kt}^{*}\|=O(n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}}h_{Tt})
where
h_{Tt}=\sqrt{\frac{T-t}{T-t+1}}
,
r_{kt}^{*}=h_{Tt}(r_{kt}-\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=t+1}^{T}r_{kt})
and
r_{kt}
is defined in equation (
).

Proof.

For (i), first, from Lemma
S3
, we can derive that
\|e^{\Delta_{k}}-I_{n}\|=O_{p}(\|\Delta_{k}\|)=O_{p}(n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}})
.
Second,
\|S_{k}\|_{\mathrm{rc}}\leq e^{\sum_{p_{k}=1}^{\ell_{k}}|\lambda_{p_{k}}|\|\varPhi_{kp_{k}}\|_{\mathrm{rc}}}=O_{p}(1)
from Assumption
B2
(i) and (ii) and thus
\|S_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O_{p}(1)
.
Third, since

R_{k}=S_{k}(e^{\Delta_{k}}-I_{n})
, by Lemmas
S1
and
S3
, we have

\|R_{g_{k}}\|\leq\|S_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|e^{\Delta_{k}}-I_{n}\|=O_{p}(1)O_{p}(\|\Delta_{k}\|)=O_{p}(n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}}).

The results of
\|R_{k}\|_{\mathrm{rc}}=O_{p}(\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}})
since
\|e^{\Delta_{k}}-I_{n}\|_{\mathrm{rc}}=O_{p}(\|\Delta_{k}\|_{\mathrm{rc}})=O_{p}(\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}})
.
Fourth, we have

\|S_{k}R_{g_{k}}\|\leq\|S_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|R_{g_{k}}\|=O_{p}(n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}}).

For (ii), first, combining (
1
) and (
2
), we have

\displaystyle U_{t}=B_{3}^{-1}E_{t}=(R_{3}+S_{3})^{-1}E_{t}

(S1)

When
k=3
, we have
r_{3t}=R_{3}U_{t}=H_{3}E_{t}
, where
H_{3}=R_{3}B_{3}^{-1}
.
Since
B_{k}
,
S_{k}
and
R_{k}
are invertible, we have

B_{3}^{-1}=(I_{n}+S_{3}^{-1}R_{3})^{-1}S_{3}^{-1}
.
Then, we have
\|S_{k}^{-1}R_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O_{p}(n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}})
which implies
\ell_{k}^{\varsigma_{k}}=o(n^{1/2})
. Thus, by the Neumann series, we have
\|(I_{n}+S_{3}^{-1}R_{3})^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(1)
,
\|B_{3}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(1)
and
\|H_{3}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O_{p}(\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{3}})
.
For (iii), consider
k=3
, and
r_{3t}^{*}=h_{Tt}(r_{3t}-\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=t+1}^{T}r_{3t}).

Note that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\operatorname{E}\|r_{3t}\|^{2}&=\operatorname{E}\sum_{i=1}^{n}r_{3t,i}^{2}=\operatorname{E}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{3,ij}\epsilon_{jt})^{2}=\operatorname{E}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}h_{3,ij}h_{3,ik}\epsilon_{jt}\epsilon_{kt}\\
&=\operatorname{E}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{3,ij}^{2}\epsilon_{jt}^{2}\leq\sqrt{\operatorname{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{3,ij}^{2})^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\operatorname{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{n}\epsilon_{j}^{4}\right]}\\
&=O(\sqrt{n}\ell_{3}^{-2\varsigma_{3}})\cdot O(\sqrt{n})=O(n\ell_{3}^{-2\varsigma_{3}}),\end{split}

by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and the fact that
\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{3,ij}^{2}\leq(\sum_{j=1}^{n}|h_{ij}|)^{2}\leq\|H_{3}\|_{\infty}^{2}=O_{p}(\ell_{3}^{-2\varsigma_{3}})
.
Thus,

\operatorname{E}\|r_{3t}\|\leq\sqrt{\operatorname{E}\|r_{3t}\|^{2}}=O(n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{3}})
. So we have

\operatorname{E}\|\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=t+1}^{T}r_{3t}\|\leq\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=t+1}^{T}\operatorname{E}\|r_{3t}\|=O(n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{3}}).

Finally,
\operatorname{E}\|r_{3t}^{*}\|=O(n^{1/2}\ell_{3}^{-\varsigma_{3}}h_{Tt})
. Similarly, we have

\operatorname{E}\|r_{1t}^{*}\|=O(n^{1/2}\ell_{1}^{-\varsigma_{1}}h_{Tt})
and
\operatorname{E}\|r_{2t}^{*}\|=O(n^{1/2}\ell_{2}^{-\varsigma_{2}}h_{Tt})
as
\rho(A)<1
.

\blacksquare

Lemma S6
.

Under Assumption
2
(i), the covariance of
m_{N}^{\mathtt{line}}(\theta_{0})
and
m_{N}^{\mathtt{quad}}(\theta_{0})
is asymptotically zero as
(n,T)\to\infty
.

Proof.

Similar to the Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

\blacksquare

Lemma S7
.

Under Assumption
B4
,
\|M_{t}\|_{\mathrm{rc}}=O_{p}\left(\ell_{n}\right)
, and
\|M_{t}\|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\ell_{n}}\right)
.

Proof.

Note that the entries of
M_{t}
are

m_{ijt}=\tfrac{1}{n}\Bigl[J_{n}Q_{t}\Bigr]_{i}^{\prime}\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{n}Q_{nt}^{\prime}J_{n}\Sigma_{t}J_{n}Q_{nt}\Bigr)^{-1}\Bigl[J_{n}Q_{t}\Bigr]_{j}

and thus

|m_{ijt}|=O_{p}\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{n}\Big\|\bigl[J_{n}Q_{t}\bigr]_{i}\Big\|\Big\|\bigl[J_{n}Q_{t}\bigr]_{j}\Big\|\Bigr)=O_{p}\Bigl(\frac{\ell_{n}}{n}\Bigr)

uniformly in
i,j
for each
t
.
Similarly, we also observe that

\sum_{j=1}^{n}m_{ijt}^{2}=O_{p}\Bigl(\frac{\ell_{n}}{n}\Bigr).

uniformly in
i
for each
t
.
So we have

\|M_{t}\|_{\mathrm{rc}}=O_{p}(\ell_{n})
,
\|M_{t}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O_{p}(\ell_{n})

and thus
\|M_{t}\|=O_{p}(\sqrt{\ell_{n}})
.

\blacksquare

Lemma
S7
indicates that the linear moments may be subject to various moment issues due to the cross-sectional dimension, which can be viewed as an extension of those presented in
Lee and Yu 2014
.

Lemma S8
.

For
n\times n
time-varying matrix
\mathcal{B}_{t}
, suppose
\mathcal{B}_{t}
satisfies
A1

O_{p}(b)
and
Q_{jt}
is the
j
-th column components of the IV
Q_{t}
, under Assumptions
1
,
B2
and
B4
:

(i)
\operatorname{E}\|r_{kt}^{*}\|^{2}=O(n^{2}h_{Tt}^{4}\ell_{k}^{-4\varsigma_{k}})
;

(ii)

\operatorname{E}|r_{kt}^{*\prime}{\mathcal{B}_{t}}r_{kt}^{*}|=O\left(nh_{Tt}^{2}\ell_{k}^{-2\varsigma_{k}}b\right)
;

(iii)

\operatorname{E}|r_{kt}^{*\prime}\mathcal{B}_{t}E_{t}^{*}|=O(nh_{Tt}^{2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}}b)
.

(iv)

\operatorname{E}|Q_{jt}^{\prime}\mathcal{B}_{t}(r_{kt}^{*}+E_{t}^{*})|=O(\sqrt{n}h_{Tt}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}}b)
.

Proof.

For (i)

\operatorname{E}\|r_{kt}\|^{2}=\operatorname{E}\left[\sum_{i}r_{kt,i}^{2}\right]^{2}=\operatorname{E}\left[\sum_{i}(\sum_{j}h_{3,ij}\epsilon_{j})^{2}\right]^{2}=\operatorname{E}(E_{t}^{\prime}H_{3}^{\prime}H_{3}E_{t})^{2}
. Note that
H_{3}^{\prime}H_{3}
satisfies
A1

O_{p}(h^{2})
with
h=\ell_{3}^{-\varsigma_{3}}
, and we have
\operatorname{E}\|r_{kt}\|^{2}=O(n^{2}h_{Tt}^{4}\ell_{k}^{-4\varsigma_{k}})
.
For (ii), taking
k=3
as an example, according to Lemmas
S1
and
S5
, we know that the dominant term is
h_{Tt}\operatorname{E}|r_{3t}^{\prime}{\mathcal{B}_{t}}r_{3t}|
. According to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\operatorname{E}|r_{kt}^{\prime}{\mathcal{B}_{t}}r_{3t}|\leq\sqrt{\operatorname{E}\|r_{kt}^{\prime}{\mathcal{B}_{t}}r_{3t}\|^{2}}=\sqrt{\operatorname{E}\|E_{t}^{\prime}H_{3}^{\prime}\mathcal{B}_{t}H_{3}E_{t}\|^{2}}
, also,
H_{3}^{\prime}\mathcal{B}_{t}H_{3}
satisfies
A1

O_{p}(b\ell_{3}^{-2\varsigma_{3}})
. Thus,
\operatorname{E}\|r_{3t}^{\prime}{\mathcal{B}_{t}}r_{3t}\|^{2}=O\left(n^{2}\ell_{3}^{-4\varsigma_{3}}b^{2}\right)
.
The result of (iii) can be proved similarly to (ii). For (iv), we know that
Q_{t}
can be expressed as a linear combination of
Y_{t-1}^{(*,-1)}
and
X_{t}^{*}
. Since that
Y_{t-1}^{(*,-1)}
and
X_{t}^{*}
are both uncorrelated with
E_{t}^{*}
, the desired result follows immediately by arguments analogous to those used for (ii) and (iii).

\blacksquare

Lemma S9
.

Under Assumption
1
,
\operatorname{Var}({M}({\Sigma_{t}})\Sigma_{t}^{-1/2}E_{t}^{*})={M}({\Sigma_{t}})+o(1)
.

Proof.

For the case of
3.2
and
3.2
, we have
\operatorname{Var}({M}({\Sigma_{t}})\Sigma_{t}^{-1/2}E_{t}^{*})={M}({\Sigma_{t}})
. Hence, we only need to investigate the case of
3.2
. Denote
H_{t}={M}({\Sigma_{t}})\Sigma_{t}^{-1/2}E_{t}^{*}
.
To prove that
\operatorname{Var}(H_{t})={M}({\Sigma_{t}})+o(1)
under the case of
3.2
where
\Sigma_{t}=\sigma_{t}^{2}I_{n}
, we first observe that the scaling matrix simplifies to
\Sigma_{t}^{-1/2}=\sigma_{t}^{-1}I_{n}
and the projection matrix reduces to the standard centering matrix
M(\Sigma_{t})=I_{n}-(\sigma_{t}^{-1}l_{n})(n\sigma_{t}^{-2})^{-1}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}l_{n})^{\prime}=J_{n}
.
Since the variance of the forward orthogonal deviation error
E_{t}^{*}
is dominated by the current period variance
\sigma_{t}^{2}I_{n}
for large
T
, specifically,
\operatorname{Var}(E_{t}^{*})=\sigma_{t}^{2}I_{n}+O((T-t)^{-1})
, the variance of the transformed error becomes

\operatorname{Var}(H_{t})=J_{n}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}I_{n})[\sigma_{t}^{2}I_{n}+o(1)](\sigma_{t}^{-1}I_{n})J_{n}=J_{n}(I_{n}+o(1))J_{n}=J_{n}+o(1)
. Given that
M(\Sigma_{t})=J_{n}
in this specification, it follows that
\operatorname{Var}(H_{t})={M}({\Sigma_{t}})+o_{p}(1)
as
T\to\infty
.

\blacksquare

Lemma S10
.

Under Assumptions
B1
-
B4
,
\operatorname{E}\|m_{N}(\theta_{0})\|=o(1)
.

Proof.

Observe that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\operatorname{E}\|m_{N}(\theta_{0})\|&\leq\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\Bigl(\operatorname{E}\|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{p}}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{Nj}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\|+\operatorname{E}\|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{q}}\mathbf{Q}_{Nj}^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\|\Bigr)\\
&=O\left((\ell_{n}^{-2\underline{\varsigma}}+\ell_{n}^{-\underline{\varsigma}})\cdot\ell_{n}^{1/2}\right)=o(1)\end{split}

where the last two relations follow by the same argument used to bound
\mathscr{A}_{1N}
and
\mathscr{A}_{2N}
in the proof of Lemma
C4
.

\blacksquare

Lemma S11
.

(i) For the MESS-type, when
j=1,...,\ell_{k}
and
k=1,2,3
, under Assumption
2
and
S_{k}
’s satisfy
A1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{kj_{0}}}-\varPhi_{kj}S_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(\|\varPhi_{kj}\|_{\mathrm{sp}})=O(1).

(ii)
\|\frac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{k_{0}}}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(\sqrt{\ell_{n}})
, where
\frac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{k_{0}}}=\left[\frac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{k_{10}}},...,\frac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{{k\ell_{k}}_{0}}}\right]
for the true estimand
\lambda_{kj_{0}}
.

Proof.

Since
\varPhi_{kj}
does not commute with
S_{k}
, the Fréchet derivative of the matrix exponential gives

\displaystyle\tfrac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{kj_{0}}}=\int_{0}^{1}e^{(1-t)\Xi_{k}}\varPhi_{kj}e^{t\Xi_{k}}ds

Because the norm of an integral is less than the integral of the norm, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\tfrac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{kj_{0}}}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}&\leq\left(\int_{0}^{1}\|e^{(1-t)\Xi_{k}}\varPhi_{kj}e^{t\Xi_{k}}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}dt\right)\leq\left(\int_{0}^{1}\|e^{(1-t)\Xi_{k}}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|\varPhi_{kj}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|e^{t\Xi_{k}}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}dt\right)\\
&\leq\|\varPhi_{kj}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}e^{(1-t)\|\Xi_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}}e^{t\|\Xi_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}}dt\right)\leq\|\varPhi_{kj}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\cdot e^{\|\Xi_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}}=O(\|\varPhi_{kj}\|_{\mathrm{sp}})=O(1).\end{split}

from Assumption
2
.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{kj_{0}}}-\varPhi_{kj}S_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\|\tfrac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{kj_{0}}}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}+\|\varPhi_{kj}S_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(\|\varPhi_{kj}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}).

\blacksquare

Appendix S2
Estimation of heteroskedastic variances

First, we explain the transformation operator
{J}({\Sigma_{t}})
derived in BGMME, which can be motivated from the approximated log-likelihood function

\displaystyle L_{nT}(\theta,\mathbf{c}_{n},\bm{\alpha}_{T},\Sigma_{t})=-\frac{nT}{2}\ln(2\pi)-\frac{nT}{2}\ln|\Sigma_{t}(\theta)|+T\bigl(\ln|(S_{1}(\lambda)|+\ln|S_{3}(\lambda)|\bigr)-\sum_{t=1}^{T}V_{t}^{c\prime}(\theta)\Sigma_{t}^{-1}(\theta)V_{t}^{c}(\theta).

The use of the approximate likelihood relies on the negligibility of
r_{t}
, which in turn permits the replacement of the true
g_{k0}
with asymptotically negligible cost.
Concentrating out
\bm{\alpha}_{T}
by the first-order condition, we have

\displaystyle L_{nT}(\theta,\mathbf{c}_{n},\Sigma_{t})=-\frac{nT}{2}\ln(2\pi)-\frac{nT}{2}\ln|\Sigma_{t}(\theta)|+T\bigl(\ln|(S_{1}(\lambda)|+\ln|S_{3}(\lambda)|\bigr)-\sum_{t=1}^{T}V_{t}^{c\prime}(\theta){J}({\Sigma_{t}})V_{t}^{c}(\theta),

(S2)

where
V_{t}^{c}(\theta)=S_{3}(\lambda)S_{1}(\lambda)Y_{t}-S_{3}\bigl((\gamma I_{n}+S_{2})Y_{t-1}+X_{t}\beta+\mathbf{c}_{n}\bigr)
and
{J}({\Sigma_{t}})=\Sigma_{t}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t}^{-1}l_{n}(l_{n}^{\prime}\Sigma_{t}^{-1}l_{n})^{-1}l_{n}^{\prime}\Sigma_{t}^{-1}
. Thus,
{J}({\Sigma_{t}})
enables the best moment conditions to mimic the score of the likelihood function, and thus it can provide the moment conditions more efficiently than the counterparts relying on the operator
J_{n}
.
The BGMME has two main advantages over MLE. First, when the model is SAR-type, it avoids evaluating the Jacobian determinant. Second, the BGMME is subject only to approximation bias from sieves, whereas MLE can suffer additional bias from the incidental-parameters problem, as documented in the dynamic panel data literature.

A Supplement to

Semi-nonparametric estimation of spatial dynamic panel data models with nonparametric spatial weights

Abhimanyu Gupta

Department of Economics, Queen’s University, Dunning Hall, 94 University Avenue, Kingston, Ontario K7L 3N6, Canada and Department of Economics, University of Essex, Wivenhoe Park, Colchester, CO4 3SQ, UK. Email: abhimanyu.g@queensu.ca.

Appendix S1
Additional lemmas

Lemma S1
.

Let
A
and
B
be
n\times n
real matrices, then
\|AB\|\leq\|A\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|B\|
or
\|AB\|\leq\|B\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|A\|
.

Proof.

This is a standard matrix norm inequality. For a formal discussion and proof, see Section 5.6 of
horn2012matrix
.

\blacksquare

Lemma S2
.

(i) For any time-varying square matrix
\mathcal{B}_{t}
, if

\sup\limits_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{1}=\sup\limits_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\infty}=O(1)
, then
\sup\limits_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(1)
.

(ii) For any time-varying square random matrix
\mathcal{B}_{t}
, depending on an index
n
. If

\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{1}=O_{p}(1)

and

\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(1)
,
then
\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O_{p}(1)

Proof.

(i)
We use the standard matrix norm inequality
\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\sqrt{\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{1}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\infty}}
. Taking the supremum over
t
yields:

\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\sup_{t}\sqrt{\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{1}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\infty}}\leq\sqrt{\left(\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{1}\right)\left(\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\infty}\right)}=O(1).

(ii)
According to the proof of (i),
let
X=\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{1}
and
Y=\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\infty}
. Then,
Z=XY=O_{p}(1)
. Since norms are non-negative,
Z\geq 0
. Therefore,
\sqrt{Z}=\sqrt{XY}=O_{p}(1)
. Hence,
0\leq\sup_{t}\|\mathcal{B}_{t}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\sqrt{XY}=O_{p}(1)
.

\blacksquare

Lemma S3
.

Let the
n\times n
matrix
A
with its typical element
a_{ij}
satisfy

\sup_{i,j}|a_{ij}|=O_{p}(d^{-\nu})
and

\sup_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|a_{ij}|=O_{p}(d^{-\nu})
for some
\nu>0
. Suppose that
n^{1/2}d^{-\nu}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, then:

\|e^{A}-I\|=O_{p}(\|A\|)=O_{p}(n^{1/2}d^{-\nu}).

Proof.
First, it is easy to conclude that
\|A\|=O_{p}(n^{1/2}d^{-\nu})
since
\sum_{j=1}^{n}|a_{ij}|^{2}\leq\left(\sup_{k}|a_{ik}|\right)\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}|a_{ij}|\right)\leq O_{p}(d^{-2\nu})
. Therefore,
\|A\|^{2}=O_{p}(nd^{-2\nu})
.
Second,
Using the triangle inequality for the Frobenius norm and the submultiplicative property, we get:

\|e^{A}-I\|\leq\|A\|+\frac{\|A^{2}\|}{2!}+\frac{\|A^{3}\|}{3!}+\cdots
.
Since
\|A^{k}\|\leq\|A\|^{k}
, this becomes:

\|e^{A}-I\|\leq\|A\|+\frac{\|A\|^{2}}{2!}+\frac{\|A\|^{3}}{3!}+\cdots=e^{\|A\|}-1
.
Since
\|A^{k}\|\leq\|A\|^{k}
, this becomes:

\|e^{A}-I\|\leq\|A\|+\frac{\|A\|^{2}}{2!}+\frac{\|A\|^{3}}{3!}+\cdots=e^{\|A\|}-1
.
Let
X_{p}=\|A\|
,
we have
e^{X_{p}}-1=X_{p}+o_{p}(1)
.
Combining above all, we conclude that
\|e^{A}-I\|=O_{p}(\|A\|)
.

\blacksquare

Lemma
S3
shows that the MESS has the same asymptotic behavior as that of the SAR.

Lemma S4
.

When
T\to\infty
,

\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}h_{Tt}^{2}=O(T)
and
\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}(1-h_{Tt}^{2})=O(\ln{T})
, where
h_{Tt}=\sqrt{\frac{T-t}{T-t+1}}
.

Proof.

Note that
h_{Tt}^{2}=1-\frac{1}{T-t+1}
, and we have
\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}h_{Tt}^{2}=O(T)-O(\ln{T})=O(T)
since
\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{1}{T-t+1}=O(\ln{T})
.

\blacksquare

Lemma S5
.

Under Assumption
B2
and
\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}}+n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
:

(i)
e^{\Delta_{k}}-I_{n}
satisfies
A1

O_{p}(\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}})
, where
\Delta_{k}
is defined in Notation
A1
(ii). Also,
R_{k}
and
S_{k}R_{k}
satisfy
A1

O_{p}(\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}})
, and
B_{k}
and
S_{k}
satisfy
A1
;

(ii)

H_{k}
satisfies
A1

O_{p}(\ell_{3}^{-\varsigma_{3}})
, where
H_{k}=R_{k}B_{k}^{-1}
;

(iii)

\operatorname{E}\|r_{kt}^{*}\|=O(n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}}h_{Tt})
where
h_{Tt}=\sqrt{\frac{T-t}{T-t+1}}
,
r_{kt}^{*}=h_{Tt}(r_{kt}-\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=t+1}^{T}r_{kt})
and
r_{kt}
is defined in equation (
).

Proof.

For (i), first, from Lemma
S3
, we can derive that
\|e^{\Delta_{k}}-I_{n}\|=O_{p}(\|\Delta_{k}\|)=O_{p}(n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}})
.
Second,
\|S_{k}\|_{\mathrm{rc}}\leq e^{\sum_{p_{k}=1}^{\ell_{k}}|\lambda_{p_{k}}|\|\varPhi_{kp_{k}}\|_{\mathrm{rc}}}=O_{p}(1)
from Assumption
B2
(i) and (ii) and thus
\|S_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O_{p}(1)
.
Third, since

R_{k}=S_{k}(e^{\Delta_{k}}-I_{n})
, by Lemmas
S1
and
S3
, we have

\|R_{g_{k}}\|\leq\|S_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|e^{\Delta_{k}}-I_{n}\|=O_{p}(1)O_{p}(\|\Delta_{k}\|)=O_{p}(n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}}).

The results of
\|R_{k}\|_{\mathrm{rc}}=O_{p}(\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}})
since
\|e^{\Delta_{k}}-I_{n}\|_{\mathrm{rc}}=O_{p}(\|\Delta_{k}\|_{\mathrm{rc}})=O_{p}(\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}})
.
Fourth, we have

\|S_{k}R_{g_{k}}\|\leq\|S_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|R_{g_{k}}\|=O_{p}(n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}}).

For (ii), first, combining (
1
) and (
2
), we have

\displaystyle U_{t}=B_{3}^{-1}E_{t}=(R_{3}+S_{3})^{-1}E_{t}

(S1)

When
k=3
, we have
r_{3t}=R_{3}U_{t}=H_{3}E_{t}
, where
H_{3}=R_{3}B_{3}^{-1}
.
Since
B_{k}
,
S_{k}
and
R_{k}
are invertible, we have

B_{3}^{-1}=(I_{n}+S_{3}^{-1}R_{3})^{-1}S_{3}^{-1}
.
Then, we have
\|S_{k}^{-1}R_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O_{p}(n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}})
which implies
\ell_{k}^{\varsigma_{k}}=o(n^{1/2})
. Thus, by the Neumann series, we have
\|(I_{n}+S_{3}^{-1}R_{3})^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(1)
,
\|B_{3}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(1)
and
\|H_{3}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O_{p}(\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{3}})
.
For (iii), consider
k=3
, and
r_{3t}^{*}=h_{Tt}(r_{3t}-\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=t+1}^{T}r_{3t}).

Note that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\operatorname{E}\|r_{3t}\|^{2}&=\operatorname{E}\sum_{i=1}^{n}r_{3t,i}^{2}=\operatorname{E}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{3,ij}\epsilon_{jt})^{2}=\operatorname{E}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}h_{3,ij}h_{3,ik}\epsilon_{jt}\epsilon_{kt}\\
&=\operatorname{E}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{3,ij}^{2}\epsilon_{jt}^{2}\leq\sqrt{\operatorname{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{3,ij}^{2})^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\operatorname{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{n}\epsilon_{j}^{4}\right]}\\
&=O(\sqrt{n}\ell_{3}^{-2\varsigma_{3}})\cdot O(\sqrt{n})=O(n\ell_{3}^{-2\varsigma_{3}}),\end{split}

by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and the fact that
\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{3,ij}^{2}\leq(\sum_{j=1}^{n}|h_{ij}|)^{2}\leq\|H_{3}\|_{\infty}^{2}=O_{p}(\ell_{3}^{-2\varsigma_{3}})
.
Thus,

\operatorname{E}\|r_{3t}\|\leq\sqrt{\operatorname{E}\|r_{3t}\|^{2}}=O(n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{3}})
. So we have

\operatorname{E}\|\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=t+1}^{T}r_{3t}\|\leq\frac{1}{T-t}\sum_{h=t+1}^{T}\operatorname{E}\|r_{3t}\|=O(n^{1/2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{3}}).

Finally,
\operatorname{E}\|r_{3t}^{*}\|=O(n^{1/2}\ell_{3}^{-\varsigma_{3}}h_{Tt})
. Similarly, we have

\operatorname{E}\|r_{1t}^{*}\|=O(n^{1/2}\ell_{1}^{-\varsigma_{1}}h_{Tt})
and
\operatorname{E}\|r_{2t}^{*}\|=O(n^{1/2}\ell_{2}^{-\varsigma_{2}}h_{Tt})
as
\rho(A)<1
.

\blacksquare

Lemma S6
.

Under Assumption
2
(i), the covariance of
m_{N}^{\mathtt{line}}(\theta_{0})
and
m_{N}^{\mathtt{quad}}(\theta_{0})
is asymptotically zero as
(n,T)\to\infty
.

Proof.

Similar to the Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

\blacksquare

Lemma S7
.

Under Assumption
B4
,
\|M_{t}\|_{\mathrm{rc}}=O_{p}\left(\ell_{n}\right)
, and
\|M_{t}\|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\ell_{n}}\right)
.

Proof.

Note that the entries of
M_{t}
are

m_{ijt}=\tfrac{1}{n}\bigl[J_{n}Q_{t}\bigr]_{i}^{\prime}\bigl(Q_{nt}^{\prime}J_{n}\Sigma_{t}J_{n}Q_{nt}\bigr)^{-1}\bigl[J_{n}Q_{t}\bigr]_{i}

and thus

|m_{ijt}|=O_{p}\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{n}\Big\|\bigl[J_{n}Q_{t}\bigr]_{i}\Big\|\Big\|\bigl[J_{n}Q_{t}\bigr]_{j}\Big\|\Bigr)=O_{p}\Bigl(\frac{\ell_{n}}{n}\Bigr)

uniformly in
i,j
for each
t
.
Similarly, we also observe that

\sum_{j=1}^{n}m_{ijt}^{2}=O_{p}\Bigl(\frac{\ell_{n}}{n}\Bigr).

uniformly in
i
for each
t
.
So we have

\|M_{t}\|_{\mathrm{rc}}=O_{p}(\ell_{n})
,
\|M_{t}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O_{p}(\ell_{n})

and
\|M_{t}\|^{2}=O_{p}(\ell_{n})
.

\blacksquare

Lemma
S7
indicates that the linear moments may be subject to various moment issues due to the cross-sectional dimension, which can be viewed as an extension of those presented in
Lee and Yu 2014
.

Lemma S8
.

For
n\times n
time-varying matrix
\mathcal{B}_{t}
, suppose
\mathcal{B}_{t}
satisfies
A1

O_{p}(b)
and
Q_{jt}
is the
j
-th column components of the IV
Q_{t}
, under Assumptions
1
,
B2
and
B4
:

(i)
\operatorname{E}\|r_{kt}^{*}\|^{2}=O(n^{2}h_{Tt}^{4}\ell_{k}^{-4\varsigma_{k}})
;

(ii)

\operatorname{E}|r_{kt}^{*\prime}{\mathcal{B}_{t}}r_{kt}^{*}|=O\left(nh_{Tt}^{2}\ell_{k}^{-2\varsigma_{k}}b\right)
;

(iii)

\operatorname{E}|r_{kt}^{*\prime}\mathcal{B}_{t}E_{t}^{*}|=O(nh_{Tt}^{2}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}}b)
.

(iv)

\operatorname{E}|Q_{jt}^{\prime}\mathcal{B}_{t}(r_{kt}^{*}+E_{t}^{*})|=O(\sqrt{n}h_{Tt}\ell_{k}^{-\varsigma_{k}}b)
.

Proof.

For (i)

\operatorname{E}\|r_{kt}\|^{2}=\operatorname{E}\left[\sum_{i}r_{kt,i}^{2}\right]^{2}=\operatorname{E}\left[\sum_{i}(\sum_{j}h_{3,ij}\epsilon_{j})^{2}\right]^{2}=\operatorname{E}(E_{t}^{\prime}H_{3}^{\prime}H_{3}E_{t})^{2}
. Note that
H_{3}^{\prime}H_{3}
satisfies
A1

O_{p}(h^{2})
with
h=\ell_{3}^{-\varsigma_{3}}
, and we have
\operatorname{E}\|r_{kt}\|^{2}=O(n^{2}h_{Tt}^{4}\ell_{k}^{-4\varsigma_{k}})
.
For (ii), taking
k=3
as an example, according to Lemmas
S1
and
S5
, we know that the dominant term is
h_{Tt}\operatorname{E}|r_{3t}^{\prime}{\mathcal{B}_{t}}r_{3t}|
. According to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\operatorname{E}|r_{kt}^{\prime}{\mathcal{B}_{t}}r_{3t}|\leq\sqrt{\operatorname{E}\|r_{kt}^{\prime}{\mathcal{B}_{t}}r_{3t}\|^{2}}=\sqrt{\operatorname{E}\|E_{t}^{\prime}H_{3}^{\prime}\mathcal{B}_{t}H_{3}E_{t}\|^{2}}
, also,
H_{3}^{\prime}\mathcal{B}_{t}H_{3}
satisfies
A1

O_{p}(b\ell_{3}^{-2\varsigma_{3}})
. Thus,
\operatorname{E}\|r_{3t}^{\prime}{\mathcal{B}_{t}}r_{3t}\|^{2}=O\left(n^{2}\ell_{3}^{-4\varsigma_{3}}b^{2}\right)
.
The result of (iii) can be proved similarly to (ii). For (iv), we know that
Q_{t}
can be expressed as a linear combination of
Y_{t-1}^{(*,-1)}
and
X_{t}^{*}
. Since that
Y_{t-1}^{(*,-1)}
and
X_{t}^{*}
are both uncorrelated with
E_{t}^{*}
, the desired result follows immediately by arguments analogous to those used for (ii) and (iii).

\blacksquare

Lemma S9
.

Under Assumption
1
,
\operatorname{Var}({M}({\Sigma_{t}})\Sigma_{t}^{-1/2}E_{t}^{*})={M}({\Sigma_{t}})+o(1)
.

Proof.

For the case of
3.2
and
3.2
, we have
\operatorname{Var}({M}({\Sigma_{t}})\Sigma_{t}^{-1/2}E_{t}^{*})={M}({\Sigma_{t}})
. Hence, we only need to investigate the case of
3.2
. Denote
H_{t}={M}({\Sigma_{t}})\Sigma_{t}^{-1/2}E_{t}^{*}
.
To prove that
\operatorname{Var}(H_{t})={M}({\Sigma_{t}})+o(1)
under the case of
3.2
where
\Sigma_{t}=\sigma_{t}^{2}I_{n}
, we first observe that the scaling matrix simplifies to
\Sigma_{t}^{-1/2}=\sigma_{t}^{-1}I_{n}
and the projection matrix reduces to the standard centering matrix
M(\Sigma_{t})=I_{n}-(\sigma_{t}^{-1}l_{n})(n\sigma_{t}^{-2})^{-1}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}l_{n})^{\prime}=J_{n}
.
Since the variance of the forward orthogonal deviation error
E_{t}^{*}
is dominated by the current period variance
\sigma_{t}^{2}I_{n}
for large
T
, specifically,
\operatorname{Var}(E_{t}^{*})=\sigma_{t}^{2}I_{n}+O((T-t)^{-1})
, the variance of the transformed error becomes

\operatorname{Var}(H_{t})=J_{n}(\sigma_{t}^{-1}I_{n})[\sigma_{t}^{2}I_{n}+o(1)](\sigma_{t}^{-1}I_{n})J_{n}=J_{n}(I_{n}+o(1))J_{n}=J_{n}+o(1)
. Given that
M(\Sigma_{t})=J_{n}
in this specification, it follows that
\operatorname{Var}(H_{t})={M}({\Sigma_{t}})+o_{p}(1)
as
T\to\infty
.

\blacksquare

Lemma S10
.

Under Assumptions
B1
-
B4
,
\operatorname{E}\|m_{N}(\theta_{0})\|=o(1)
.

Proof.

Observe that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\operatorname{E}\|m_{N}(\theta_{0})\|&\leq\frac{1}{n(T-1)}\Bigl(\operatorname{E}\|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{p}}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{P}_{Nj}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\|+\operatorname{E}\|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{q}}\mathbf{Q}_{Nj}^{\prime}\mathbf{J}_{N}\mathbf{V}_{N}^{*}\|\Bigr)\\
&=O\left((\ell_{n}^{-2\underline{\varsigma}}+\ell_{n}^{-\underline{\varsigma}})\cdot\ell_{n}^{1/2}\right)=o(1)\end{split}

where the last two relations follow by the same argument used to bound
\mathscr{A}_{1N}
and
\mathscr{A}_{2N}
in the proof of Lemma
C4
.

\blacksquare

Lemma S11
.

(i) For the MESS, when
j=1,...,\ell_{k}
and
k=1,2,3
, under Assumption
2
and
S_{k}
’s satisfy
A1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{kj_{0}}}-\varPhi_{kj}S_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(\|\varPhi_{kj}\|_{\mathrm{sp}})=O(1).

(ii)
\|\frac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{k_{0}}}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(\sqrt{\ell_{n}})
, where
\frac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{k_{0}}}=\left[\frac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{k_{10}}},...,\frac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{{k\ell_{k}}_{0}}}\right]
for the true estimand
\lambda_{kj_{0}}
.

Proof.

Since
\varPhi_{kj}
does not commute with
S_{k}
, the Fréchet derivative of the matrix exponential gives

\displaystyle\tfrac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{kj_{0}}}=\int_{0}^{1}e^{(1-t)\Xi_{k}}\varPhi_{kj}e^{t\Xi_{k}}ds

Because the norm of an integral is less than the integral of the norm, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\tfrac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{kj_{0}}}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}&\leq\left(\int_{0}^{1}\|e^{(1-t)\Xi_{k}}\varPhi_{kj}e^{t\Xi_{k}}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}dt\right)\leq\left(\int_{0}^{1}\|e^{(1-t)\Xi_{k}}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|\varPhi_{kj}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\|e^{t\Xi_{k}}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}dt\right)\\
&\leq\|\varPhi_{kj}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}e^{(1-t)\|\Xi_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}}e^{t\|\Xi_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}}dt\right)\leq\|\varPhi_{kj}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\cdot e^{\|\Xi_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}}=O(\|\varPhi_{kj}\|_{\mathrm{sp}})=O(1).\end{split}

from Assumption
2
.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{kj_{0}}}-\varPhi_{kj}S_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\leq\|\tfrac{\partial S_{k}}{\partial\lambda_{kj_{0}}}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}+\|\varPhi_{kj}S_{k}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=O(\|\varPhi_{kj}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}).

\blacksquare

Appendix S2
Estimation of heteroskedastic variances

First, we explain the transformation operator
{J}({\Sigma_{t}})
derived in BGMME, which can be motivated from the approximated log-likelihood function

\displaystyle L_{nT}(\theta,\mathbf{c}_{n},\bm{\alpha}_{T},\Sigma_{t})=-\frac{nT}{2}\ln(2\pi)-\frac{nT}{2}\ln|\Sigma_{t}(\theta)|+T\bigl(\ln|(S_{1}(\lambda)|+\ln|S_{3}(\lambda)|\bigr)-\sum_{t=1}^{T}V_{t}^{c\prime}(\theta)\Sigma_{t}^{-1}(\theta)V_{t}^{c}(\theta).

The use of the approximate likelihood relies on the negligibility of
r_{t}
, which in turn permits the replacement of the true
g_{k0}
with asymptotically negligible cost.
Concentrating out
\bm{\alpha}_{T}
by the first-order condition, we have

\displaystyle L_{nT}(\theta,\mathbf{c}_{n},\Sigma_{t})=-\frac{nT}{2}\ln(2\pi)-\frac{nT}{2}\ln|\Sigma_{t}(\theta)|+T\bigl(\ln|(S_{1}(\lambda)|+\ln|S_{3}(\lambda)|\bigr)-\sum_{t=1}^{T}V_{t}^{c\prime}(\theta){J}({\Sigma_{t}})V_{t}^{c}(\theta),

(S2)

where
V_{t}^{c}(\theta)=S_{3}(\lambda)S_{1}(\lambda)Y_{t}-S_{3}\bigl((\gamma I_{n}+S_{2})Y_{t-1}+X_{t}\beta+\mathbf{c}_{n}\bigr)
and
{J}({\Sigma_{t}})=\Sigma_{t}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t}^{-1}l_{n}(l_{n}^{\prime}\Sigma_{t}^{-1}l_{n})^{-1}l_{n}^{\prime}\Sigma_{t}^{-1}
. Thus,
{J}({\Sigma_{t}})
enables the best moment conditions to mimic the score of the likelihood function, and thus it can provide the moment conditions more efficiently than the counterparts relying on the operator
J_{n}
.
The BGMME has two main advantages over MLE. First, when the model is SAR, it avoids evaluating the Jacobian determinant. Second, the BGMME is subject only to approximation bias from sieves, whereas MLE can suffer additional bias from the incidental-parameters problem, as documented in the dynamic panel data literature.
